Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Remove `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units` — migrate to `config_key_value`

## Context

These three `[rustc]` fields duplicate what `[cargo.config_key_value]` can express via
`cargo --config`. We confirmed they produce identical binary sizes. Since we're the only
users, we can remove them without deprecation. `[rustc]` stays with `build_std` and `flags`.

## Step 1: Remove fields from `RustcConfig` and delete `OptLevel` enum

**File:** `src/types.rs`

- Delete `OptLevel` enum entirely (lines 16-31)
- Remove from `RustcConfig`: `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units`
- Keep: `build_std`, `flags`

## Step 2: Remove RUSTFLAGS generation for removed fields

**File:** `src/cargo_build.rs`

- Remove `OptLevel` from the import on line 12
- Remove the `opt_level` match block (lines 300-310)
- Remove the `lto` block (lines 312-314)
- Remove the `codegen_units` block (lines 316-318)

## Step 3: Remove from field registry, validation, and scalar parsing

**File:** `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs`

- Remove 3 entries from `FIELD_REGISTRY`: `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units`
- Remove 3 `validate_value` match arms for those keys
- Remove `parse_scalar_value` special cases for `rustc.lto` and `rustc.codegen_units`
  (the `rustc.opt_level` comment can go too — it was just "keep as string")
- Remove/update affected unit tests:
  - `validate_key_valid_scalar`: remove `rustc.lto` assertion
  - `validate_value_lto`: delete
  - `validate_value_codegen_units`: delete
  - `validate_value_opt_level`: delete
  - `set_nested_scalar`: uses `rustc.lto` — change to use another bool field or remove
  - `set_codegen_units_as_integer`: delete

## Step 4: Update set.rs tests

**File:** `src/ts_cmd/set.rs`

- Delete: `set_rustc_lto`, `set_rustc_opt_level`, `set_codegen_units`

## Step 5: Update unset.rs tests

**File:** `src/ts_cmd/unset.rs`

- `unset_nested_field`: uses `rustc.lto` and `rustc.opt_level` — rewrite to use

## Step 6: Update cmd/ts.rs doc comments

**File:** `src/cmd/ts.rs`

- Line 82: Change `"rustc.lto"` example to `"cargo.profile"`

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::io::Write;

     7→use crate::find_paths::{

    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};

    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    16→    if had_stale {
    17→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    18→    }
    19→}
    20→
    21→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    22→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    23→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    24→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    25→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    26→        return true;
    27→    }
    28→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
    29→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
    30→        .lines()
    31→        .map(|l| l.trim())
    32→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
    33→        .all(|l| {
    34→            l == "fn main() {"
    35→                || l == "}"
    36→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
    37→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
    38→        });
    39→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
    40→}
    41→
    42→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
    43→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
    44→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
    45→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
    46→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
    47→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
    48→    {
    49→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
    50→        return true;
    51→    }
    52→    false
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Result of a successful build
    56→pub struct BuildResult {
    57→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
    58→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
    59→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success
    63→pub fn build_package(pkg_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {
    64→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    65→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
    66→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
    67→
    68→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
    69→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
    70→
    71→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
    72→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
    73→
    74→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    75→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
    76→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    77→
    78→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
    79→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    80→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
    81→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    82→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
    83→        (Some(s), td)
    84→    } else {
    85→        (None, None)
    86→    };
    87→
    88→    // Compute paths
    89→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
    90→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    91→        None => workspace.join("target"),
    92→    };
    93→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
    94→        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref())
    95→    } else {
    96→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)
    97→    };
    98→
    99→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build
   100→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   101→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   102→        println!("Building {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
   103→
   104→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   105→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   106→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   107→        if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
   108→            println!(
   109→                "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
   110→                pkg_name
   111→            );
   112→        }
   113→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   114→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   115→        }
   116→
   117→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;
   118→        cmd.arg("build");
   119→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   120→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   121→
   122→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   123→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   124→
   125→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
   126→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   127→    } else {
   128→        println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   129→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   130→        cmd.arg("build");
   131→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   132→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   133→        if release {
   134→            cmd.arg("--release");
   135→        }
   136→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   137→    };
   138→
   139→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   140→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   141→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    if !status.success() {
   145→        bail!("cargo build failed");
   146→    }
   147→
   148→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   149→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   150→    Ok(BuildResult {
   151→        binary_path,
   152→        target_base,
   153→    })
   154→}
   155→
   156→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   157→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   158→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   159→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   160→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   161→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   162→
   163→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   164→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   165→
   166→    let binary_path = get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, true);
   167→    let target_base = workspace.join("target");
   168→
   169→    println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   170→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   171→    cmd.arg("build");
   172→    cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   173→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   174→    cmd.arg("--release");
   175→    let status = cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?;
   176→
   177→    if !status.success() {
   178→        bail!("cargo build failed");
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   182→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   183→    Ok(BuildResult {
   184→        binary_path,
   185→        target_base,
   186→    })
   187→}
   188→
   189→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   190→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   191→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   192→
   193→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   194→        lines.push(format!(
   195→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   196→            crate_name, arg
   197→        ));
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   201→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   202→    Ok(())
   203→}
   204→
   205→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
   206→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   207→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
   208→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
   209→
   210→    // BuildStd requires nightly
   211→    let has_build_std = !spec.rustc.build_std.is_empty();
   212→
   213→    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly
   214→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   215→
   216→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   217→}
   218→
   219→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   220→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {
   221→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   222→
   223→    if requires_nightly(spec) {
   224→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   225→    }
   226→
   227→    Ok(cmd)
   228→}
   229→
   230→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command
   231→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   232→    cmd: &mut Command,
   233→    spec: &Spec,
   234→    workspace: &Path,
   235→    release: bool,
   236→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   237→) -> Result<()> {
   238→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   239→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   240→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   241→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   245→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   246→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   247→    {
   248→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   249→    }
   250→
   251→    // Handle cargo config
   252→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   253→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   254→        match profile {
   255→            Profile::Release => {
   256→                cmd.arg("--release");
   257→            }
   258→            Profile::Debug => {
   259→                // Debug is default, no flag needed
   260→            }
   261→        }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   265→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   266→    }
   267→
   268→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   269→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   270→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   271→    }
   272→
   273→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   274→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   275→    }
   276→
   277→    // Inject --config key=value pairs
   278→    for (key, value) in &spec.cargo.config_key_value {
   279→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   280→    }
   281→
   282→    // If no profile in spec but release flag passed, use release
   283→    if !has_profile && release {
   284→        cmd.arg("--release");
   285→    }
   286→
   287→    // Collect rustc flags
   288→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   289→
   290→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   291→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   292→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   293→    {
   294→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   295→    }
   296→
   297→    // Handle rustc config
   298→    let rustc = &spec.rustc;
   299→
   300→    if let Some(level) = &rustc.opt_level {
   301→        let lvl = match level {
   302→            OptLevel::O0 => "0",
   303→            OptLevel::O1 => "1",
   304→            OptLevel::O2 => "2",
   305→            OptLevel::O3 => "3",
   306→            OptLevel::Os => "s",
   307→            OptLevel::Oz => "z",
   308→        };
   309→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C opt-level={}", lvl));
   310→    }
   311→
   312→    if let Some(true) = rustc.lto {
   313→        rustc_flags.push("-C lto=true".to_string());
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    if let Some(n) = rustc.codegen_units {
   317→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C codegen-units={}", n));
   318→    }
   319→
   320→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   321→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   322→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   323→    {
   324→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   325→    }
   326→
   327→    if !rustc.build_std.is_empty() {
   328→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   329→        let crates_str = rustc.build_std.join(",");
   330→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   331→    }
   332→
   333→    for flag in &rustc.flags {
   334→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   335→    }
   336→
   337→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   338→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   339→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   340→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   341→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   342→        };
   343→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   344→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   345→
   346→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   347→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   348→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   349→
   350→        let mut f =
   351→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   352→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   353→
   354→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   355→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   356→            version_script_path.display()
   357→        ));
   358→    }
   359→
   360→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   361→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   362→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   363→    }
   364→
   365→    Ok(())
   366→}
   367→
```

```
     1→//! toml_edit helpers for surgical tspec editing that preserves comments/formatting.
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use toml_edit::{Array, DocumentMut, Item, Value};
     5→
     6→/// Whether a field holds a scalar, an array, or a table.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
     8→pub enum FieldKind {
     9→    Scalar,
    10→    Array,
    11→    Table,
    12→}
    13→
    14→/// Registry entry: (dotted key path, kind).
    15→const FIELD_REGISTRY: &[(&str, FieldKind)] = &[
    16→    ("panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    17→    ("strip", FieldKind::Scalar),
    18→    ("cargo.profile", FieldKind::Scalar),
    19→    ("cargo.target_triple", FieldKind::Scalar),
    20→    ("cargo.target_json", FieldKind::Scalar),
    21→    ("cargo.target_dir", FieldKind::Scalar),
    22→    ("cargo.unstable", FieldKind::Array),
    23→    ("cargo.config_key_value", FieldKind::Table),
    24→    ("rustc.opt_level", FieldKind::Scalar),
    25→    ("rustc.lto", FieldKind::Scalar),
    26→    ("rustc.codegen_units", FieldKind::Scalar),
    27→    ("rustc.build_std", FieldKind::Array),
    28→    ("rustc.flags", FieldKind::Array),
    29→    ("linker.args", FieldKind::Array),
    30→];
    31→
    32→/// Validate that a key is in the registry and return its kind.
    33→/// Also accepts table sub-keys like `cargo.config_key_value."profile.release.opt-level"`.
    34→pub fn validate_key(key: &str) -> Result<FieldKind> {
    35→    for &(k, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    36→        if k == key {
    37→            return Ok(kind);
    38→        }
    39→    }
    40→    // Check if it's a table sub-key
    41→    if parse_table_key(key).is_some() {
    42→        return Ok(FieldKind::Table);
    43→    }
    44→    let valid_keys: Vec<&str> = FIELD_REGISTRY.iter().map(|(k, _)| *k).collect();
    45→    bail!(
    46→        "unknown key: {} (valid keys: {})",
    47→        key,
    48→        valid_keys.join(", ")
    49→    )
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Parse a key that may reference a sub-key within a Table field.
    53→/// E.g., `cargo.config_key_value."profile.release.opt-level"` → Some(("cargo.config_key_value", "profile.release.opt-level"))
    54→/// Also accepts unquoted sub-keys: `cargo.config_key_value.profile.release.opt-level` → same result.
    55→/// Returns None if the key doesn't start with a known Table field prefix.
    56→pub fn parse_table_key(key: &str) -> Option<(&str, &str)> {
    57→    for &(prefix, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    58→        if kind != FieldKind::Table {
    59→            continue;
    60→        }
    61→        if let Some(rest) = key.strip_prefix(prefix)
    62→            && let Some(sub_key) = rest.strip_prefix('.')
    63→        {
    64→            if sub_key.is_empty() {
    65→                return None;
    66→            }
    67→            // Strip surrounding quotes if present
    68→            let sub_key = sub_key
    69→                .strip_prefix('"')
    70→                .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix('"'))
    71→                .unwrap_or(sub_key);
    72→            if sub_key.is_empty() {
    73→                return None;
    74→            }
    75→            return Some((prefix, sub_key));
    76→        }
    77→    }
    78→    None
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Validate a value for enum-constrained fields.
    82→/// For unconstrained fields (strings, arrays), accepts anything.
    83→pub fn validate_value(key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    84→    match key {
    85→        "panic" => match value {
    86→            "unwind" | "abort" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    87→            _ => bail!(
    88→                "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
    89→                value
    90→            ),
    91→        },
    92→        "strip" => match value {
    93→            "none" | "debuginfo" | "symbols" => Ok(()),
    94→            _ => bail!(
    95→                "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
    96→                value
    97→            ),
    98→        },
    99→        "cargo.profile" => match value {
   100→            "debug" | "release" => Ok(()),
   101→            _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", value),
   102→        },
   103→        "rustc.opt_level" => match value {
   104→            "0" | "1" | "2" | "3" | "s" | "z" => Ok(()),
   105→            _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", value),
   106→        },
   107→        "rustc.lto" => match value {
   108→            "true" | "false" | "yes" | "no" | "1" | "0" => Ok(()),
   109→            _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", value),
   110→        },
   111→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
   112→            value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| {
   113→                anyhow::anyhow!("invalid codegen_units: {} (expected integer)", value)
   114→            })?;
   115→            Ok(())
   116→        }
   117→        _ => Ok(()),
   118→    }
   119→}
   120→
   121→/// Parse a key into (optional table, field).
   122→/// "panic" -> (None, "panic")
   123→/// "rustc.lto" -> (Some("rustc"), "lto")
   124→fn parse_key(key: &str) -> (Option<&str>, &str) {
   125→    match key.split_once('.') {
   126→        Some((table, field)) => (Some(table), field),
   127→        None => (None, key),
   128→    }
   129→}
   130→
   131→/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.
   132→/// Booleans -> bool, integers -> i64, everything else -> string.
   133→fn parse_scalar_value(key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {
   134→    // For rustc.lto, always parse as boolean
   135→    if key == "rustc.lto" {
   136→        return match raw {
   137→            "true" | "yes" | "1" => Value::from(true),
   138→            _ => Value::from(false),
   139→        };
   140→    }
   141→
   142→    // For rustc.codegen_units, always parse as integer
   143→    if key == "rustc.codegen_units"
   144→        && let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>()
   145→    {
   146→        return Value::from(n);
   147→    }
   148→
   149→    // For rustc.opt_level, keep as string (since "0","1",etc. are enum variants)
   150→    Value::from(raw)
   151→}
   152→
   153→/// Get the existing array for a field, or an empty array if it doesn't exist.
   154→fn get_existing_array(doc: &DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Array {
   155→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   156→    match table_name {
   157→        Some(table) => doc
   158→            .get(table)
   159→            .and_then(|t| t.get(field))
   160→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   161→            .cloned()
   162→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   163→        None => doc
   164→            .get(field)
   165→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   166→            .cloned()
   167→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   168→    }
   169→}
   170→
   171→/// Set an array value into the document at the given key path.
   172→fn set_array_in_doc(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, arr: Array) {
   173→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   174→    match table_name {
   175→        Some(table) => {
   176→            ensure_table(doc, table);
   177→            doc[table][field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   178→        }
   179→        None => {
   180→            doc[field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   181→        }
   182→    }
   183→}
   184→
   185→/// Ensure a table exists in the document.
   186→fn ensure_table(doc: &mut DocumentMut, table: &str) {
   187→    if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   188→        doc[table] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   189→    }
   190→}
   191→
   192→// === Public API: takes &[String] from shell args, no string parsing ===
   193→
   194→/// Set a field from string args. Scalars take `values[0]`, arrays take all values.
   195→pub fn set_field(
   196→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   197→    key: &str,
   198→    values: &[String],
   199→    kind: FieldKind,
   200→) -> Result<()> {
   201→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   202→
   203→    match kind {
   204→        FieldKind::Scalar => {
   205→            if values.len() != 1 {
   206→                bail!(
   207→                    "scalar field '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
   208→                    key,
   209→                    values.len()
   210→                );
   211→            }
   212→            let val = parse_scalar_value(key, &values[0]);
   213→            match table_name {
   214→                Some(table) => {
   215→                    ensure_table(doc, table);
   216→                    doc[table][field] = Item::Value(val);
   217→                }
   218→                None => {
   219→                    doc[field] = Item::Value(val);
   220→                }
   221→            }
   222→        }
   223→        FieldKind::Array => {
   224→            let mut arr = Array::new();
   225→            for v in values {
   226→                arr.push(v.as_str());
   227→            }
   228→            set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   229→        }
   230→        FieldKind::Table => {
   231→            bail!(
   232→                "use set_table_value() for table field '{}'; set_field() does not handle tables",
   233→                key
   234→            );
   235→        }
   236→    }
   237→
   238→    Ok(())
   239→}
   240→
   241→/// Add items to an array field. Appends by default, or inserts at `index`.
   242→/// Deduplicates on append; insert adds at position without dedup.
   243→pub fn add_items(
   244→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   245→    key: &str,
   246→    values: &[String],
   247→    index: Option<usize>,
   248→) -> Result<()> {
   249→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   250→
   251→    match index {
   252→        Some(idx) => {
   253→            if idx > arr.len() {
   254→                bail!(
   255→                    "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   256→                    idx,
   257→                    key,
   258→                    arr.len()
   259→                );
   260→            }
   261→            // Insert at position (no dedup — user explicitly chose position)
   262→            for (offset, v) in values.iter().enumerate() {
   263→                arr.insert(idx + offset, v.as_str());
   264→            }
   265→        }
   266→        None => {
   267→            // Append with dedup
   268→            let existing: Vec<String> = arr
   269→                .iter()
   270→                .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(|s| s.to_string()))
   271→                .collect();
   272→            for v in values {
   273→                if !existing.contains(v) {
   274→                    arr.push(v.as_str());
   275→                }
   276→            }
   277→        }
   278→    }
   279→
   280→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   281→    Ok(())
   282→}
   283→
   284→/// Remove items from an array field by value.
   285→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if all items are removed.
   286→pub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {
   287→    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   288→
   289→    let mut new_arr = Array::new();
   290→    for item in arr.iter() {
   291→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   292→            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)
   293→        {
   294→            new_arr.push(s);
   295→        }
   296→    }
   297→
   298→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);
   299→    Ok(())
   300→}
   301→
   302→/// Remove an item from an array field by index.
   303→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if the last item is removed.
   304→pub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {
   305→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   306→
   307→    if index >= arr.len() {
   308→        bail!(
   309→            "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   310→            index,
   311→            key,
   312→            arr.len()
   313→        );
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    arr.remove(index);
   317→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   318→    Ok(())
   319→}
   320→
   321→/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.
   322→/// Does not remove the containing table, even if it becomes empty.
   323→pub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   324→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   325→
   326→    match table_name {
   327→        Some(table) => {
   328→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {
   329→                tbl.remove(field);
   330→            }
   331→        }
   332→        None => {
   333→            doc.remove(field);
   334→        }
   335→    }
   336→
   337→    Ok(())
   338→}
   339→
   340→/// Smart-parse a raw value string into a toml_edit Value for table entries.
   341→/// Booleans → bool, integers → i64, everything else → string.
   342→fn parse_smart_value(raw: &str) -> Value {
   343→    match raw {
   344→        "true" => Value::from(true),
   345→        "false" => Value::from(false),
   346→        _ => {
   347→            if let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>() {
   348→                Value::from(n)
   349→            } else {
   350→                Value::from(raw)
   351→            }
   352→        }
   353→    }
   354→}
   355→
   356→/// Set a value in a table field (e.g., `cargo.config_key_value`).
   357→/// `table_path` is the dotted path to the table (e.g., "cargo.config_key_value").
   358→/// `sub_key` is the key within that table (e.g., "profile.release.opt-level").
   359→/// `raw_value` is the string value to set (auto-parsed to bool/int/string).
   360→pub fn set_table_value(
   361→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   362→    table_path: &str,
   363→    sub_key: &str,
   364→    raw_value: &str,
   365→) -> Result<()> {
   366→    let (parent, table_name) = parse_key(table_path);
   367→    let val = parse_smart_value(raw_value);
   368→
   369→    match parent {
   370→        Some(p) => {
   371→            ensure_table(doc, p);
   372→            // Ensure the nested table exists
   373→            if doc[p].get(table_name).is_none() {
   374→                doc[p][table_name] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   375→            }
   376→            doc[p][table_name][sub_key] = Item::Value(val);
   377→        }
   378→        None => {
   379→            if doc.get(table_name).is_none() {
   380→                doc[table_name] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   381→            }
   382→            doc[table_name][sub_key] = Item::Value(val);
   383→        }
   384→    }
   385→
   386→    Ok(())
   387→}
   388→
   389→/// Remove a single key from a table field.
   390→pub fn unset_table_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, table_path: &str, sub_key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   391→    let (parent, table_name) = parse_key(table_path);
   392→
   393→    match parent {
   394→        Some(p) => {
   395→            if let Some(Item::Table(parent_tbl)) = doc.get_mut(p)
   396→                && let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = parent_tbl.get_mut(table_name)
   397→            {
   398→                tbl.remove(sub_key);
   399→            }
   400→        }
   401→        None => {
   402→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table_name) {
   403→                tbl.remove(sub_key);
   404→            }
   405→        }
   406→    }
   407→
   408→    Ok(())
   409→}

   415→    // Helper to make a Vec<String> from string literals
   416→    fn vs(items: &[&str]) -> Vec<String> {

   418→    }

   829→    #[test]
   830→    fn parse_table_key_quoted() {
   831→        let result = parse_table_key("cargo.config_key_value.\"profile.release.opt-level\"");
   832→        assert_eq!(
   833→            result,
   834→            Some(("cargo.config_key_value", "profile.release.opt-level"))
   835→        );
```

```
     1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a value in a tspec using toml_edit (preserves comments/formatting)
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
     6→
     7→use super::edit::{self, FieldKind};
     8→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     9→
    10→/// Set a field in a tspec (scalar or replace entire array) and save in place
    11→pub fn set_value(
    12→    project_root: &Path,
    13→    package: Option<&str>,
    14→    key: &str,
    15→    values: &[String],
    16→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    17→) -> Result<()> {
    18→    let workspace = project_root;
    19→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    20→
    21→    // Resolve tspec path (existing or new)
    22→    let output_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    23→        Some(path) => path,
    24→        None => {
    25→            let base_name = match tspec {
    26→                Some(t) => t
    27→                    .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    28→                    .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    29→                    .unwrap_or(t),
    30→                None => "tspec",
    31→            };
    32→            package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX))
    33→        }
    34→    };
    35→
    36→    // Validate key and value
    37→    let kind = edit::validate_key(key)?;
    38→
    39→    // Handle Table fields with sub-keys
    40→    if kind == FieldKind::Table {
    41→        if let Some((table_path, sub_key)) = edit::parse_table_key(key) {
    42→            if values.len() != 1 {
    43→                anyhow::bail!(
    44→                    "table sub-key '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
    45→                    key,
    46→                    values.len()
    47→                );
    48→            }
    49→
    50→            let content = if output_path.exists() {
    51→                std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    52→                    .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?
    53→            } else {
    54→                String::new()
    55→            };
    56→
    57→            let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
    58→                .parse()
    59→                .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    60→
    61→            edit::set_table_value(&mut doc, table_path, sub_key, &values[0])?;
    62→
    63→            std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
    64→                .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    65→
    66→            println!(
    67→                "Saved {}",
    68→                output_path
    69→                    .strip_prefix(workspace)
    70→                    .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    71→                    .display()
    72→            );
    73→            return Ok(());
    74→        } else {
    75→            anyhow::bail!(
    76→                "'ts set' on table field '{}' requires a sub-key, e.g. {}.\"key\"",
    77→                key,
    78→                key
    79→            );
    80→        }
    81→    }
    82→
    83→    // Validate enum constraints for scalar fields
    84→    if kind == FieldKind::Scalar {
    85→        if values.len() != 1 {
    86→            anyhow::bail!(
    87→                "scalar field '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
    88→                key,
    89→                values.len()
    90→            );
    91→        }
    92→        edit::validate_value(key, &values[0])?;
    93→    }
    94→
    95→    // Read existing content or start empty
    96→    let content = if output_path.exists() {
    97→        std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    98→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?
    99→    } else {
   100→        String::new()
   101→    };
   102→
   103→    // Parse, edit, write
   104→    let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
   105→        .parse()
   106→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
   107→
   108→    edit::set_field(&mut doc, key, values, kind)?;
   109→
   110→    std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
   111→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
   112→
   113→    println!(
   114→        "Saved {}",
   115→        output_path
   116→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
   117→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
   118→            .display()
   119→    );
   120→
   121→    Ok(())
   122→}
   123→
```

```
     1→//! `tspec ts unset` - Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments/formatting)
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
     6→
     7→use super::edit;
     8→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     9→
    10→/// Remove a field from a tspec
    11→pub fn unset_value(
    12→    project_root: &Path,
    13→    package: Option<&str>,
    14→    key: &str,
    15→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    16→) -> Result<()> {
    17→    let workspace = project_root;
    18→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    19→
    20→    let output_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    21→        Some(path) => path,
    22→        None => bail!("no tspec found to modify"),
    23→    };
    24→
    25→    // Validate the key
    26→    let kind = edit::validate_key(key)?;
    27→
    28→    // Read, parse, edit, write
    29→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    30→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    31→
    32→    let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
    33→        .parse()
    34→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    35→
    36→    if kind == edit::FieldKind::Table {
    37→        if let Some((table_path, sub_key)) = edit::parse_table_key(key) {
    38→            edit::unset_table_value(&mut doc, table_path, sub_key)?;
    39→        } else {
    40→            // Bare table name (e.g., "cargo.config_key_value") — remove entire table
    41→            edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key)?;
    42→        }
    43→    } else {
    44→        edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key)?;
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
    48→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    49→
    50→    println!(
    51→        "Saved {}",
    52→        output_path
    53→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    54→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    55→            .display()
    56→    );
    57→
    58→    Ok(())
    59→}
    60→
    61→#[cfg(test)]
    62→mod tests {
    63→    use crate::test_constants::SUFFIX;
    64→    use crate::tspec::load_spec;
    65→    use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
    66→
    67→    use super::*;
    68→
    69→    fn unset_in_file(content: &str, key: &str) -> (tempfile::TempDir, std::path::PathBuf, String) {
    70→        let dir = tempfile::TempDir::new().unwrap();
    71→        let path = dir.path().join(format!("tspec{}", SUFFIX));
    72→        std::fs::write(&path, content).unwrap();
    73→
    74→        let mut doc: DocumentMut = content.parse().unwrap();
    75→        let kind = edit::validate_key(key).unwrap();
    76→        if kind == edit::FieldKind::Table {
    77→            if let Some((table_path, sub_key)) = edit::parse_table_key(key) {
    78→                edit::unset_table_value(&mut doc, table_path, sub_key).unwrap();
    79→            } else {
    80→                edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key).unwrap();
    81→            }
    82→        } else {
    83→            edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key).unwrap();
    84→        }
    85→        let output = doc.to_string();
    86→        std::fs::write(&path, &output).unwrap();
    87→
    88→        (dir, path, output)
    89→    }
    90→
    91→    #[test]
    92→    fn unset_toplevel_field() {
    93→        let input = "panic = \"abort\"\nstrip = \"symbols\"\n";
    94→        let (_dir, path, _) = unset_in_file(input, "panic");
    95→        let spec = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
    96→        assert_eq!(spec.panic, None);
    97→        assert_eq!(spec.strip, Some(crate::options::StripMode::Symbols));
    98→    }
    99→
   100→    #[test]
   101→    fn unset_nested_field() {
   102→        let input = "[rustc]\nlto = true\nopt_level = \"3\"\n";
   103→        let (_dir, path, _) = unset_in_file(input, "rustc.lto");
   104→        let spec = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
   105→        assert_eq!(spec.rustc.lto, None);
   106→        assert_eq!(spec.rustc.opt_level, Some(crate::types::OptLevel::O3));
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    #[test]
   110→    fn unset_array_field() {
   111→        let input = "[linker]\nargs = [\"-static\"]\n";
   112→        let (_dir, path, _) = unset_in_file(input, "linker.args");
   113→        let spec = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
   114→        assert!(spec.linker.args.is_empty());
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    #[test]
   118→    fn unset_preserves_comments() {
   119→        // Comment is attached to strip (which stays), not to panic (which is removed)
   120→        let input = "panic = \"abort\"\n# Keep this\nstrip = \"symbols\"\n";
   121→        let (_dir, _, output) = unset_in_file(input, "panic");
   122→        assert!(output.contains("# Keep this"));
   123→        assert!(output.contains("strip = \"symbols\""));
   124→    }
   125→
   126→    #[test]
   127→    fn unset_unknown_key_errors() {
   128→        let result = edit::validate_key("nonexistent");
   129→        assert!(result.is_err());
   130→    }
   131→
   132→    // --- Table field (config_key_value) tests ---
   133→
   134→    #[test]
   135→    fn unset_config_kv_subkey() {
   136→        let input = "[cargo.config_key_value]\n\"profile.release.opt-level\" = \"s\"\n\"profile.release.lto\" = true\n";
   137→        let (_dir, path, _) = unset_in_file(
   138→            input,
   139→            "cargo.config_key_value.\"profile.release.opt-level\"",
   140→        );
   141→        let spec = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
   142→        assert!(
   143→            spec.cargo
   144→                .config_key_value
   145→                .get("profile.release.opt-level")
   146→                .is_none()
   147→        );
   148→        assert_eq!(
   149→            spec.cargo.config_key_value.get("profile.release.lto"),
   150→            Some(&crate::types::ConfigValue::Bool(true))
   151→        );
   152→    }
   153→
   154→    #[test]
   155→    fn unset_config_kv_entire_table() {
   156→        let input = "[cargo.config_key_value]\n\"profile.release.opt-level\" = \"s\"\n\"profile.release.lto\" = true\n";
   157→        let (_dir, path, _) = unset_in_file(input, "cargo.config_key_value");
   158→        let spec = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
   159→        assert!(spec.cargo.config_key_value.is_empty());
   160→    }
   161→}
```

```
     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::{Args, Subcommand};
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::ts_cmd;
     8→
     9→/// Manage translation specs
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct TsCmd {
    12→    #[command(subcommand)]
    13→    command: TsCommands,
    14→}
    15→
    16→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    17→pub enum TsCommands {
    18→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    19→    List {
    20→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→        package: Option<String>,
    23→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→        all: bool,
    26→    },
    27→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    28→    Show {
    29→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    30→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    31→        package: Option<String>,
    32→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    33→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    34→        all: bool,
    35→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    36→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    37→        tspec: Option<String>,
    38→    },
    39→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    40→    Hash {
    41→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    42→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    43→        package: Option<String>,
    44→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    45→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    46→        all: bool,
    47→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    48→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    49→        tspec: Option<String>,
    50→    },
    51→    /// Create a new tspec file
    52→    New {
    53→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    54→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    55→        name: String,
    56→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    57→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    58→        package: Option<String>,
    59→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    60→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    61→        from: Option<String>,
    62→    },
    63→    /// Set a field in a tspec (scalar value or replace entire array)
    64→    ///
    65→    /// For scalars: tspec ts set key value
    66→    /// For arrays: tspec ts set key val1 val2 ...
    67→    Set {
    68→        /// Field key (e.g., "rustc.lto", "linker.args")
    69→        key: String,
    70→        /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
    71→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    72→        value: Vec<String>,
    73→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    74→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    75→        package: Option<String>,
    76→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    77→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    78→        tspec: Option<String>,
    79→    },
    80→    /// Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments)
    81→    Unset {
    82→        /// Key to remove (e.g., "rustc.lto", "panic", "linker.args")
    83→        key: String,
    84→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    85→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    86→        package: Option<String>,
    87→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    88→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    89→        tspec: Option<String>,
    90→    },
    91→    /// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
    92→    Add {
    93→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
    94→        key: String,
    95→        /// Items to add
    96→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    97→        value: Vec<String>,
    98→        /// Insert at this index instead of appending
    99→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   100→        index: Option<usize>,
   101→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   102→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   103→        package: Option<String>,
   104→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   105→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   106→        tspec: Option<String>,
   107→    },
   108→    /// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   109→    Remove {
   110→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
   111→        key: String,
   112→        /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   113→        #[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   114→        value: Vec<String>,
   115→        /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   116→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   117→        index: Option<usize>,
   118→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   119→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   120→        package: Option<String>,
   121→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   122→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   123→        tspec: Option<String>,
   124→    },
   125→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
   126→    Backup {
   127→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   128→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   129→        package: Option<String>,
   130→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   131→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   132→        tspec: Option<String>,
   133→    },
   134→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
   135→    Restore {
   136→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   137→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   138→        package: Option<String>,
   139→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
   140→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   141→        tspec: String,
   142→    },
   143→}
   144→
   145→impl Execute for TsCmd {
   146→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   147→        match &self.command {
   148→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
   149→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
   150→            }
   151→            TsCommands::Show {
   152→                package,
   153→                all,
   154→                tspec,
   155→            } => {
   156→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   157→            }
   158→            TsCommands::Hash {
   159→                package,
   160→                all,
   161→                tspec,
   162→            } => {
   163→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   164→            }
   165→            TsCommands::New {
   166→                name,
   167→                package,
   168→                from,
   169→            } => {
   170→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   171→            }
   172→            TsCommands::Set {
   173→                key,
   174→                value,
   175→                package,
   176→                tspec,
   177→            } => {
   178→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   179→                    project_root,
   180→                    package.as_deref(),
   181→                    key,
   182→                    value,
   183→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   184→                )?;
   185→            }
   186→            TsCommands::Unset {
   187→                key,
   188→                package,
   189→                tspec,
   190→            } => {
   191→                ts_cmd::unset_value(project_root, package.as_deref(), key, tspec.as_deref())?;
   192→            }
   193→            TsCommands::Add {
   194→                key,
   195→                value,
   196→                index,
   197→                package,
   198→                tspec,
   199→            } => {
   200→                ts_cmd::add_value(
   201→                    project_root,
   202→                    package.as_deref(),
   203→                    key,
   204→                    value,
   205→                    *index,
   206→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   207→                )?;
   208→            }
   209→            TsCommands::Remove {
   210→                key,
   211→                value,
   212→                index,
   213→                package,
   214→                tspec,
   215→            } => {
   216→                ts_cmd::remove_value(
   217→                    project_root,
   218→                    package.as_deref(),
   219→                    key,
   220→                    value,
   221→                    *index,
   222→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   223→                )?;
   224→            }
   225→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   226→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   227→            }
   228→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   229→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   230→            }
   231→        }
   232→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   233→    }
   234→}
   235→
```

```
     7→fn test_data(name: &str) -> PathBuf {

     9→        .join("tests/data")

    13→#[test]

    16→
    17→    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));
    18→
    19→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some(Profile::Release));
```

```
     6→
     7→`[cargo.config_key_value]` is now implemented, providing a general-purpose mechanism for
     8→passing `--config KEY=VALUE` args to cargo. This enables per-package profile overrides
     9→without bleeding settings through RUSTFLAGS.
    10→
    11→### Open questions
    12→
    13→- **Auto-scoping:** Should tspec automatically scope config keys with
    14→  `profile.*.package.<name>` so users don't have to write the full path? Currently users
    15→  write exact cargo config keys — no magic.
    16→- **`[rustc]` field migration:** Several `[rustc]` fields (`opt_level`, `lto`,
    17→  `codegen_units`) duplicate what cargo profiles express. These could migrate to
    18→  `config_key_value` entries, but that's a separate follow-up. The existing fields work
    19→  fine via RUSTFLAGS for now.
    20→- **High-level fields (`panic`, `strip`):** These expand to both cargo and rustc flags.
    21→  Moving them to `config_key_value` would lose the multi-flag expansion. They likely stay
    22→  as top-level fields.
    23→
    24→### Finding: RUSTFLAGS vs --config produce same-size but non-identical binaries
    25→
    26→Tested with `tspec-build` library crate using two equivalent specs:
    27→- `tspec.opt.ts.toml` — uses `rustc.opt_level = "z"` and `rustc.codegen_units = 1` (RUSTFLAGS)
    28→- `tspec.opt-kv.ts.toml` — uses `[cargo.config_key_value]` with equivalent `--config` args
    29→
    30→Results:
    31→- Binary sizes: **identical** (1,117,880 bytes both)
    32→- Binary content: **differs** (~72K of ~1.1M bytes, ~6.5%)
    33→- Changing `target_dir` alone does NOT cause differences (confirmed byte-identical)
    34→- The difference is solely from RUSTFLAGS `-C opt-level=z` vs `--config 'profile.release.opt-level="z"'`
    35→
    36→The two mechanisms produce equivalently optimized but not bit-identical binaries.
    37→Cause is unclear — possibly different flag ordering, different cargo-internal rustc
    38→invocation paths, or non-deterministic codegen. Not a practical concern since the
    39→optimization effect is the same.
    40→
    41→### Status
    42→
    43→Noting for future reference. No immediate action needed.
    44→
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/ts_cmd/edit.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.\n/// Booleans -> bool, integers -> i64, everything else -> string.\nfn parse_scalar_value(key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {\n    // For rustc.lto, always parse as boolean\n    if key == \"rustc.lto\" {\n        return match raw {\n            \"true\" | \"yes\" | \"1\" => Value::from(true),\n            _ => Value::from(false),\n        };\n    }\n\n    // For rustc.codegen_units, always parse as integer\n    if key == \"rustc.codegen_units\"\n        && let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>()\n    {\n        return Value::from(n);\n    }\n\n    // For rustc.opt_level, keep as string (since \"0\",\"1\",etc. are enum variants)\n    Value::from(raw)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.\n/// All scalar fields are stored as strings.\nfn parse_scalar_value(_key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {\n    Value::from(raw)\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
```

```json
  "old_string": "### Status\n\nNoting for future reference. No immediate action needed.",
  "new_string": "### Status\n\nDone — see next section.\n\n## 20260216 - Remove `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units` — migrate to `config_key_value`\n\n### Context\n\nThese three `[rustc]` fields duplicate what `[cargo.config_key_value]` can express via\n`cargo --config`. We confirmed they produce identical binary sizes. Since we're the only\nusers, we can remove them without deprecation. `[rustc]` section stays with `build_std`\nand `flags`.\n\n### Changes\n\n1. **types.rs:** Deleted `OptLevel` enum; removed `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units`\n   from `RustcConfig` (kept `build_std`, `flags`)\n2. **cargo_build.rs:** Removed RUSTFLAGS generation for the three fields; removed\n   `OptLevel` import\n3. **ts_cmd/edit.rs:** Removed 3 `FIELD_REGISTRY` entries, 3 `validate_value` arms,\n   `parse_scalar_value` special cases; updated/deleted affected unit tests\n4. **ts_cmd/set.rs:** Deleted `set_rustc_lto`, `set_rustc_opt_level`, `set_codegen_units` tests\n5. **ts_cmd/unset.rs:** Rewrote `unset_nested_field` test to use `rustc.build_std`/`flags`\n6. **cmd/ts.rs:** Changed `\"rustc.lto\"` doc examples to `\"cargo.profile\"`\n7. **Spec files:** Migrated `opt_level`/`codegen_units` to `[cargo.config_key_value]`\n   in `tspec.release.ts.toml`, `tspec.musl.ts.toml`, `tspec-build/tspec.opt.ts.toml`\n8. **Test fixtures:** Migrated `tests/data/minimal.toml`; updated `tests/tspec_test.rs`\n   `load_minimal_spec` to check `config_key_value` instead of `rustc.opt_level`\n\n### Status\n\nIn progress."
}
```

> AGENT

All done. Here's a summary of what was changed:

**Version:** Bumped to `0.11.0-dev1`

**Removed from `src/types.rs`:**
- `OptLevel` enum (6 variants)
- `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units` fields from `RustcConfig`

**Removed from `src/cargo_build.rs`:**
- `OptLevel` import and RUSTFLAGS generation for the three fields

**Updated `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs`:**
- Removed 3 `FIELD_REGISTRY` entries, 3 `validate_value` arms, `parse_scalar_value` special cases
- Deleted tests: `validate_value_lto`, `validate_value_codegen_units`, `set_codegen_units_as_integer`
- Rewrote tests: `set_nested_scalar`, `unset_nested`, `unset_keeps_empty_table`, `unset_nonexistent_is_ok`, `parse_table_key_not_table`

**Updated `src/ts_cmd/set.rs`:** Deleted `set_rustc_lto`, `set_rustc_opt_level`, `set_codegen_units` tests

**Updated `src/ts_cmd/unset.rs`:** Rewrote `unset_nested_field` to use `rustc.build_std`/`flags`

**Updated `src/ts_cmd/add.rs`:** Fixed `scalar_key_rejected` test (was using `rustc.lto`)

**Updated `src/cmd/ts.rs`:** Changed `"rustc.lto"` doc examples to `"cargo.profile"`

**Updated `src/tspec.rs`:** Fixed `save_and_load_roundtrip` test (was using `lto: Some(true)`)

**Migrated spec files** (`tspec.release.ts.toml`, `tspec.musl.ts.toml`, `tspec-build/tspec.opt.ts.toml`): Moved `opt_level`/`codegen_units` to `[cargo.config_key_value]`

**Updated test fixtures:** `tests/data/minimal.toml` and `tests/tspec_test.rs`

**Updated notes:** `todo.md` and `chores-5.md`

**Verification:** 194 unit tests + 8 integration tests pass, clippy clean, fmt clean, installed new binary and re-verified.

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

> AGENT

Passes in isolation. This is a pre-existing race condition — the two env-var tests (`resolve_spec_path_none_with_env` and `resolve_spec_path_none_without_env`) both mutate `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` and race when run in parallel with other test binaries. It's not related to our changes.

When `tspec test` runs both packages, the tspec-build tests can collide with the tspec test binary running concurrently, since `set_var`/`remove_var` affect the whole process. This is a known Rust testing pitfall with `unsafe` env var mutation. Not caused by this migration.

> DEVELOPER

Hmm, over the last few days I've had it fail periodically but it wasn't reproducible, now you've presented a plausible reason. But that feels like a major bug with the test infracture. What can we do about it?

> AGENT

The root cause is that `std::env::set_var`/`remove_var` are process-wide, and Rust runs tests on multiple threads within the same process. Two tests mutating `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` concurrently will race.

The options are:

1. **Static Mutex** — zero new dependencies, wrap the env-var tests so they run one at a time
2. **`serial_test` crate** — adds a dependency but gives a clean `#[serial]` attribute
3. **Refactor** — make `resolve_spec_path` take an env-reader function/closure, so tests don't touch real env vars at all

Option 3 is the cleanest long-term (no mutex, no flakiness by construction), but it's more invasive. Option 1 is the smallest fix. What's your preference?

> DEVELOPER

Seems to me it should be impossible that, probably many ways to handle this but the most obvious is if a test is modifying a TSPEC_SPEC_FILE (a xxx.ts.toml I assume) it should own that and use it as it sees fit. I'd lean to the trivial solution of the test get's a copy or creates it's own so there is no shared state. My other goto method for any shared object message passing and there is only ever one enity that changes. Using a mutex is "easy" to forget to do. Thoughts?

> AGENT

You're absolutely right. The problem is `resolve_spec_path()` reads `std::env::var("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE")` internally, forcing tests to mutate shared process state. The fix is to make the env value a parameter so each test owns its own data.

Let me look at the full implementation.

     1→use serde::Deserialize;
     2→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     3→
     4→/// Minimal spec representation — only what we need for build.rs
     5→#[derive(Deserialize, Default)]
     6→struct Spec {
     7→    #[serde(default)]
     8→    linker: LinkerConfig,
     9→}
    10→
    11→#[derive(Deserialize, Default)]
    12→struct LinkerConfig {
    13→    #[serde(default)]
    14→    args: Vec<String>,
    15→}
    16→
    17→/// Emit `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` directives from linker.args in a tspec spec.
    18→///
    19→/// With a path, reads the spec file directly (relative to `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`).
    20→/// This makes `cargo build` work without tspec for packages that need linker args:
    21→/// ```no_run
    22→/// tspec_build::emit_linker_flags_from(Some("tspec.ts.toml"));
    23→/// ```
    24→///
    25→/// With `None`, reads the spec file path from the `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` environment
    26→/// variable (set by tspec before invoking cargo). This is a no-op if the variable
    27→/// is not set, allowing the build.rs to work with both tspec and plain cargo:
    28→/// ```no_run
    29→/// tspec_build::emit_linker_flags_from(None);
    30→/// ```
    31→pub fn emit_linker_flags_from(spec_path: Option<&str>) {
    32→    let (path, from_env) = match resolve_spec_path(spec_path) {
    33→        Some(result) => result,
    34→        None => return, // No spec_path and no env var — nothing to do
    35→    };
    36→
    37→    let bin_name = std::env::var("CARGO_PKG_NAME").expect("CARGO_PKG_NAME not set");
    38→
    39→    let args = read_linker_args(&path);
    40→
    41→    for arg in &args {
    42→        println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={bin_name}={arg}");
    43→    }
    44→
    45→    // Rebuild if the spec file changes
    46→    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={}", path.display());
    47→    if from_env {
    48→        println!("cargo:rerun-if-env-changed=TSPEC_SPEC_FILE");
    49→    }
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Resolve the spec file path. Returns (path, from_env) or None if no path available.
    53→fn resolve_spec_path(spec_path: Option<&str>) -> Option<(PathBuf, bool)> {
    54→    match spec_path {
    55→        Some(p) => {
    56→            let manifest_dir =
    57→                std::env::var("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR").expect("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR not set");
    58→            Some((Path::new(&manifest_dir).join(p), false))
    59→        }
    60→        None => {
    61→            let path = std::env::var("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE").ok()?;
    62→            Some((PathBuf::from(path), true))
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→}
    66→
    67→/// Read linker.args from a spec file. Returns empty vec on any error.
    68→fn read_linker_args(path: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    69→    let content = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
    70→        Ok(c) => c,
    71→        Err(_) => return Vec::new(),
    72→    };
    73→    let spec: Spec = match toml::from_str(&content) {
    74→        Ok(s) => s,
    75→        Err(_) => return Vec::new(),
    76→    };
    77→    spec.linker.args
    78→}

Returns (path, from_env) or None if no path available.\nfn resolve_spec_path(spec_path: Option<&str>) -> Option<(PathBuf, bool)> {\n    match spec_path {\n        Some(p) => {\n            let manifest_dir =\n                std::env::var(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\").expect(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR not set\");\n            Some((Path::new(&manifest_dir).join(p), false))\n        }\n        None => {\n            let path = std::env::var(\"TSPEC_SPEC_FILE\").ok()?;\n            Some((PathBuf::from(path), true))\n        }\n    }\n}",
```json
  "new_string": "/// Resolve the spec file path. Returns (path, from_env) or None if no path available.\nfn resolve_spec_path(spec_path: Option<&str>) -> Option<(PathBuf, bool)> {\n    let manifest_dir = std::env::var(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\").ok();\n    let env_spec = std::env::var(\"TSPEC_SPEC_FILE\").ok();\n    resolve_spec_path_inner(spec_path, manifest_dir.as_deref(), env_spec.as_deref())\n}\n\n/// Pure logic for spec path resolution — no env var access.\nfn resolve_spec_path_inner(\n    spec_path: Option<&str>,\n    manifest_dir: Option<&str>,\n    env_spec_file: Option<&str>,\n) -> Option<(PathBuf, bool)> {\n    match spec_path {\n        Some(p) => {\n            let dir = manifest_dir.expect(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR not set\");\n            Some((Path::new(dir).join(p), false))\n        }\n        None => {\n            let path = env_spec_file?;\n            Some((PathBuf::from(path), true))\n        }\n    }\n}"
}
```

Everything passes — tests, clippy, fmt. The fix: extracted `resolve_spec_path_inner()` that takes env values as parameters instead of reading them directly. Tests call the inner function with their own data — no shared mutable state, no race, impossible to flake.

> AGENT

There it is — `tspec 0.11.0-dev2 TEST SUMMARY` right in the header line.

> DEVELOPER

Explain to me why using xx_inner works, I think I know but want you to tell me.

> AGENT

The `resolve_spec_path_inner` function takes the env var values as parameters instead of reading them itself. This means:

- **`resolve_spec_path`** (the real entry point) reads `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` from the process environment, then passes them to `_inner`.
- **`resolve_spec_path_inner`** does the actual logic using only its parameters — it never touches `std::env`.

Tests call `_inner` directly with whatever values they want:
```rust
resolve_spec_path_inner(None, Some("/fake/manifest"), Some("/tmp/test.ts.toml"))
resolve_spec_path_inner(None, Some("/fake/manifest"), None)
```

Each test owns its own data on the stack. No `set_var`, no `remove_var`, no shared mutable state. Two tests running on different threads can't interfere because they're just passing different arguments to a pure function.

The general principle: push side effects (env var reads, file I/O, network) to the edges, keep the core logic pure and parameterized. Then tests exercise the logic without touching the real world.

> DEVELOPER

I agree, but soon we going to add probably several "test" packages, and we're going to need to be able to "modify" them and "restore" to their original. For that case I'd lean towards having each test make a private copy in a "temp/", thoughts?

> AGENT

Agreed. For integration tests that need to build/modify real packages, a temp copy is the right approach. Each test copies the fixture package into its own `tempdir()`, modifies freely, and the temp dir cleans up on drop. No shared state, no restore step, tests can run in parallel.

The `_inner` pattern is for when you're testing pure logic that happens to read env vars or config. The temp copy pattern is for when tests need to mutate a real filesystem layout (Cargo.toml, spec files, source files) and run actual builds against them. Different problems, different tools.

> DEVELOPER

Capture this race condition to chore-5, I didn't know about it and I suspect others might not either. List the solutiions we dicussed and then our "proposed" solutions.
then commit!

```
     1→# Chores-5
     2→
     3→## 20260216 - Future: config_key_value and rustc field migration
     4→
     5→### Context
     6→
     7→`[cargo.config_key_value]` is now implemented, providing a general-purpose mechanism for
     8→passing `--config KEY=VALUE` args to cargo. This enables per-package profile overrides
     9→without bleeding settings through RUSTFLAGS.
    10→
    11→### Open questions
    12→
    13→- **Auto-scoping:** Should tspec automatically scope config keys with
    14→  `profile.*.package.<name>` so users don't have to write the full path? Currently users
    15→  write exact cargo config keys — no magic.
    16→- **`[rustc]` field migration:** Several `[rustc]` fields (`opt_level`, `lto`,
    17→  `codegen_units`) duplicate what cargo profiles express. These could migrate to
    18→  `config_key_value` entries, but that's a separate follow-up. The existing fields work
    19→  fine via RUSTFLAGS for now.
    20→- **High-level fields (`panic`, `strip`):** These expand to both cargo and rustc flags.
    21→  Moving them to `config_key_value` would lose the multi-flag expansion. They likely stay
    22→  as top-level fields.
    23→
    24→### Finding: RUSTFLAGS vs --config produce same-size but non-identical binaries
    25→
    26→Tested with `tspec-build` library crate using two equivalent specs:
    27→- `tspec.opt.ts.toml` — uses `rustc.opt_level = "z"` and `rustc.codegen_units = 1` (RUSTFLAGS)
    28→- `tspec.opt-kv.ts.toml` — uses `[cargo.config_key_value]` with equivalent `--config` args
    29→
    30→Results:
    31→- Binary sizes: **identical** (1,117,880 bytes both)
    32→- Binary content: **differs** (~72K of ~1.1M bytes, ~6.5%)
    33→- Changing `target_dir` alone does NOT cause differences (confirmed byte-identical)
    34→- The difference is solely from RUSTFLAGS `-C opt-level=z` vs `--config 'profile.release.opt-level="z"'`
    35→
    36→The two mechanisms produce equivalently optimized but not bit-identical binaries.
    37→Cause is unclear — possibly different flag ordering, different cargo-internal rustc
    38→invocation paths, or non-deterministic codegen. Not a practical concern since the
    39→optimization effect is the same.
    40→
    41→### Status
    42→
    43→Done — see next section.
    44→
    45→## 20260216 - Remove `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units` — migrate to `config_key_value`
    46→
    47→### Context
    48→
    49→These three `[rustc]` fields duplicate what `[cargo.config_key_value]` can express via
    50→`cargo --config`. We confirmed they produce identical binary sizes. Since we're the only
    51→users, we can remove them without deprecation. `[rustc]` section stays with `build_std`
    52→and `flags`.
    53→
    54→### Changes
    55→
    56→1. **types.rs:** Deleted `OptLevel` enum; removed `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units`
    57→   from `RustcConfig` (kept `build_std`, `flags`)
    58→2. **cargo_build.rs:** Removed RUSTFLAGS generation for the three fields; removed
    59→   `OptLevel` import
    60→3. **ts_cmd/edit.rs:** Removed 3 `FIELD_REGISTRY` entries, 3 `validate_value` arms,
    61→   `parse_scalar_value` special cases; updated/deleted affected unit tests
    62→4. **ts_cmd/set.rs:** Deleted `set_rustc_lto`, `set_rustc_opt_level`, `set_codegen_units` tests
    63→5. **ts_cmd/unset.rs:** Rewrote `unset_nested_field` test to use `rustc.build_std`/`flags`
    64→6. **cmd/ts.rs:** Changed `"rustc.lto"` doc examples to `"cargo.profile"`
    65→7. **Spec files:** Migrated `opt_level`/`codegen_units` to `[cargo.config_key_value]`
    66→   in `tspec.release.ts.toml`, `tspec.musl.ts.toml`, `tspec-build/tspec.opt.ts.toml`
    67→8. **Test fixtures:** Migrated `tests/data/minimal.toml`; updated `tests/tspec_test.rs`
    68→   `load_minimal_spec` to check `config_key_value` instead of `rustc.opt_level`
    69→
    70→### Status
    71→
    72→In progress.
    73→
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/notes/chores-5.md",
  "old_string": "### Status\n\nIn progress.",
  "new_string": "### Status\n\nDone.\n\n## 20260217 - Fix: test race condition with `std::env::set_var` / `remove_var`\n\n### Problem\n\n`tspec test` intermittently failed on the `resolve_spec_path_none_with_env` test in\n`tspec-build`. The failure was not reproducible when running `tspec test -p tspec-build`\nalone — only when both packages ran together via `tspec test`.\n\n### Root cause\n\nRust runs tests in parallel on multiple threads within the same process.\n`std::env::set_var()` and `std::env::remove_var()` are process-wide — they mutate\nshared global state. Two tests that manipulate the same env var (`TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`)\nrace each other:\n\n- `resolve_spec_path_none_with_env` — sets `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`, calls function, removes it\n- `resolve_spec_path_none_without_env` — removes `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`, asserts `None`\n\nIf the \"without\" test runs between the set and remove of the \"with\" test, it sees the\nvar and fails. Or vice versa. This is a well-known Rust testing pitfall — `set_var` is\nmarked `unsafe` in Rust 2024 edition precisely because of this.\n\n### Solutions considered\n\n1. **Static Mutex** — wrap env-var tests so they run one at a time. Works but easy to\n   forget for new tests, and doesn't prevent the class of bug.\n2. **`serial_test` crate** — adds `#[serial]` attribute. Clean syntax but adds a\n   dependency and still relies on developers remembering to annotate.\n3. **`--test-threads=1`** — heavy-handed, slows down all tests.\n4. **`_inner` pattern (chosen)** — extract a pure function that takes env values as\n   parameters. The public function reads env vars and delegates. Tests call `_inner`\n   directly with their own values — no shared state, no race, impossible to flake.\n5. **Temp directory copies (for future integration tests)** — when tests need to\n   modify real package layouts (Cargo.toml, spec files, source), each test copies\n   the fixture into its own `tempdir()`. No shared filesystem state, parallel-safe,\n   cleanup is automatic via `Drop`.\n\n### Principle\n\nPush side effects (env var reads, file I/O) to the edges. Keep core logic pure and\nparameterized. Tests exercise the logic without touching the real world. For filesystem\nmutations, each test owns a private copy.\n\n### Changes\n\n- **tspec-build/src/lib.rs:** Split `resolve_spec_path` into a thin env-reading wrapper\n  and `resolve_spec_path_inner` that takes `manifest_dir` and `env_spec_file` as\n  `Option<&str>` parameters. Rewrote all three `resolve_spec_path_*` tests and\n  `emit_from_reads_spec_file` to call `_inner` directly — no `set_var`/`remove_var`.\n\n### Status\n\nDone."
}
```

    18→## 20260202 - Should tspec augment or replace cargo
    19→
    20→> Tspec could either augment cargo by providing additional functionality on top of it, or it could replace cargo entirely by offering a new way to manage Rust projects. The decision depends on the specific needs of the project and the desired user experience.
    21→```
    22→
    23→
    24→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
    25→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
    26→references.
    27→
    28→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    29→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
    30→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
    31→
    32→Examples:
    33→
    34→# Todo
    35→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
    36→- Fix bug Y [1]
    37→
    38→# Done
    39→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
    40→
