```
Perfect! I have all the key files. Let me compile a comprehensive report of my findings.

## Comprehensive Analysis of Package Selection and Flags

I've thoroughly examined all the requested components. Here's the complete picture:

---

### 1. **Compare Command Struct and Flags** (`src/cmd/compare.rs`)

**Struct Definition (lines 12-30):**
```rust
#[derive(Args)]
pub struct CompareCmd {
    /// Package to compare (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    pub positional: Option<String>,
    /// Package to compare (defaults to current directory)
    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    pub package: Option<String>,
    /// Compare all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    pub workspace: bool,
    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) (defaults to tspec* pattern)
    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub fail_fast: bool,
}
```

**Key flags:**
- `-w`/`--workspace`: Forces all-packages mode even from a package directory
- `-t`/`--tspec`: Accepts multiple spec file patterns (via `num_args = 1..`), can be repeated
- `-p`/`--package` or positional: Single package selection
- `--fail-fast`: Stops on first error in all-packages mode

---

### 2. **Compare Execute Implementation** (lines 32-68)

```rust
impl Execute for CompareCmd {
    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
        let resolved = if self.workspace {
            None
        } else {
            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
                None => current_package_name(),
            }
        };

        match resolved {
            None => {
                // All-packages mode
                if !self.tspec.is_empty() {
                    anyhow::bail!(
                        "-t/--tspec cannot be used in all-packages mode. Each package uses its own tspecs."
                    );
                }
                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
                let results = compare_all(&workspace, self.fail_fast);
                Ok(print_compare_summary(&results))
            }
            Some(pkg_name) => {
                // Single package mode
                let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&pkg_name))?;
                let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
                let spec_paths = if self.tspec.is_empty() {
                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec).unwrap_or_default()
                } else {
                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?
                };
                let results = compare_specs(&pkg_name, &spec_paths)?;
                print_comparison(&pkg_name, &results);
                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Resolution logic:**
1. If `-w` is set → `None` (all-packages mode)
2. Otherwise, check positional arg or `-p` flag → resolve to package name
3. If neither exists → use current directory package name or `None` (workspace root)
4. `-t` is forbidden in all-packages mode (each package uses its own tspecs)

---

### 3. **Build, Run, Test Command Structs**

#### **BuildCmd** (`src/cmd/build.rs`, lines 13-36)
```rust
#[derive(Args)]
pub struct BuildCmd {
    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    pub positional: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    pub package: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    pub workspace: bool,
    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    pub tspec: Option<String>,  // Single string, not Vec
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub release: bool,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub strip: bool,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub fail_fast: bool,
}
```

**Key difference:** `-t`/`--tspec` accepts a **single string**, not a Vec (unlike compare).

#### **BuildCmd Execute** (lines 38-72)
```rust
impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
        let resolved = if self.workspace {
            None
        } else {
            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
                None => current_package_name(),
            }
        };

        match resolved {
            None => {
                // Build all packages
                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
                let results = build_all(
                    &workspace,
                    self.tspec.as_deref(),
                    self.release,
                    self.strip,
                    self.fail_fast,
                );
                Ok(print_summary(&results))
            }
            Some(name) => {
                let result = build_package(&name, self.tspec.as_deref(), self.release)?;
                if self.strip {
                    strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
                }
                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
            }
        }
    }
}
```

#### **RunCmd** (`src/cmd/run.rs`, lines 14-37)
```rust
#[derive(Args)]
pub struct RunCmd {
    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    pub positional: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    pub package: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    pub workspace: bool,
    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    pub tspec: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub release: bool,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub strip: bool,
    #[arg(last = true)]
    pub args: Vec<String>,  // Pass-through args after --
}
```

**RunCmd Execute** (lines 39-69): Same resolution logic as build, but:
- All-packages mode calls `run_all()` (ignores `args`)
- Single package mode builds, optionally strips, then runs with args via `run_binary()`

#### **TestCmd** (`src/cmd/test.rs`, lines 12-32)
```rust
#[derive(Args)]
pub struct TestCmd {
    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    pub positional: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    pub package: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    pub workspace: bool,
    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    pub tspec: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub release: bool,
    #[arg(short, long)]
    pub fail_fast: bool,
}
```

**TestCmd Execute** (lines 34-64): Same resolution pattern, calls `test_all()` or `test_package()`.

---

### 4. **Batch Operations in all.rs**

#### **build_all()** (lines 26-71)
```rust
pub fn build_all(
    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    tspec: Option<&str>,
    release: bool,
    strip: bool,
    fail_fast: bool,
) -> Vec<OpResult>
```

**Process:**
1. Iterates over `workspace.buildable_members()` (excludes BuildTool kind)
2. For each member, calls `build_package(&member.name, tspec, release)`
3. If strip is enabled and member has a binary, calls `strip_binary()`
4. Collects results, respects `fail_fast` flag
5. Returns `Vec<OpResult>` with success/message/size

#### **run_all()** (lines 74-117)
```rust
pub fn run_all(
    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    tspec: Option<&str>,
    release: bool,
    strip: bool,
) -> Vec<OpResult>
```

**Process:**
1. Iterates over `workspace.runnable_members()` (App kind only, with binaries)
2. Builds each package, optionally strips
3. Runs each binary with empty args `&[]`
4. Non-zero exit codes are **not treated as failures** (line 93)
5. Only build/run errors count as failures

#### **test_all()** (lines 120-258)
```rust
pub fn test_all(
    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    tspec: Option<&str>,
    release: bool,
    fail_fast: bool,
) -> Vec<OpResult>
```

**Complex logic with two paths:**

1. **Regular packages** (lines 129-157):
   - Excludes Test kind packages
   - Calls `test_package(&member.name, tspec, release)`
   - Respects `fail_fast`

2. **Test packages** (lines 160-255):
   - Builds the test package
   - Finds executables ending in `-tests` in target/{debug,release}
   - Runs each binary individually
   - Reports success/failure per binary
   - Respects `fail_fast`

#### **compare_all()** (lines 370-411)
```rust
pub fn compare_all(workspace: &WorkspaceInfo, fail_fast: bool) -> Vec<CompareResult>
```

**Process:**
1. Iterates over `buildable_members()` filtered to those with `has_binary`
2. Calls `find_tspecs(&member.path, &[])` (empty patterns = defaults)
3. Calls `compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths)`
4. Returns `Vec<CompareResult>` (contains both OpResult and spec results)
5. Respects `fail_fast`

---

### 5. **Workspace Discovery** (`src/workspace.rs`)

#### **PackageKind enum** (lines 11-23)
```rust
pub enum PackageKind {
    App,       // apps/* - runnable apps
    Lib,       // libs/* - libraries
    Tool,      // tools/* - utility binaries
    Test,      // */tests - test binaries (special)
    BuildTool, // xtask, tspec, xt - excluded from build/run/test
}
```

#### **WorkspaceInfo struct** (lines 35-38)
```rust
pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    pub root: PathBuf,
    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
}
```

#### **Discovery methods** (lines 40-105)

**discover()** (lines 42-80):
- Runs `cargo metadata --no-deps`
- Calls `metadata.workspace_packages()` to get packages
- Detects if it's a POP (single-package) via `packages.len() == 1`
- Classifies each package (POPs always App; multi-package uses `classify_package()`)
- Returns populated `WorkspaceInfo`

**buildable_members()** (lines 83-88):
- Filters `members` excluding `PackageKind::BuildTool`
- Returns `Vec<&PackageMember>`

**runnable_members()** (lines 91-96):
- Filters to packages with `has_binary && kind == PackageKind::App`
- Used by `run_all()`

**test_members()** (lines 99-104):
- Filters to `PackageKind::Test` only
- Used by `test_all()` for special -tests packages

#### **classify_package()** (lines 108-137)

Classification rules (in order):
1. Root workspace package → App
2. Named `tspec`, `xt`, or `xtask` → BuildTool
3. Path contains `/tests` or name ends with `-tests` → Test
4. Path contains `apps/` → App
5. Path contains `libs/` → Lib
6. Path contains `tools/` → Tool
7. Default (root-level members) → Lib

---

### 6. **resolve_package_arg()** (`src/cmd/mod.rs`, lines 35-45)

```rust
pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    }
}
```

**Logic:**
1. `find_project_root()` - locates workspace root
2. `find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)` - resolves pkg (path or name) to a directory
3. `get_package_name(&pkg_dir)` - extracts `[package]` name from Cargo.toml
4. Returns `Some(name)` if package found, `None` if error (e.g., at workspace root without [package])

#### **current_package_name()** (lines 69-76)
```rust
pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
        return None;  // Workspace root with no [package]
    }
    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
}
```

**Logic:**
- Gets current working directory
- If Cargo.toml exists and is NOT a POP, returns None (workspace root mode)
- Otherwise tries to get package name from cwd
- Used as fallback when no `-p` or positional arg provided

---

### Summary of Resolution Order

**All commands (build, run, test, compare) follow identical logic:**

```
1. If -w/--workspace flag set → None (all-packages mode)
2. Else if positional arg provided → resolve_package_arg(positional)
3. Else if -p/--package provided → resolve_package_arg(-p value)
4. Else → current_package_name() (cwd-based)
5. If result is None → execute_all() (batch mode)
6. If result is Some(name) → execute single package
```

**Key constraints:**
- `-t`/`--tspec` is **forbidden in all-packages mode** for compare (validation at line 47)
- Compare accepts multiple `-t` values (Vec<String>), build/run/test accept single (Option<String>)
- BuildTool packages excluded from all operations
- Test packages have special handling in test_all() (finds -tests binaries)
- Run mode filters to App kind only

    60→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    61→            }
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→}
    65→
```

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{compare_all, print_compare_summary};
     8→use crate::compare::{compare_specs, print_comparison};
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    11→
    12→/// Compare specs for a package (size only)
    13→#[derive(Args)]
    14→pub struct CompareCmd {
    15→    /// Package to compare (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    16→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    17→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    18→    /// Package to compare (defaults to current directory)
    19→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    20→    pub package: Option<String>,
    21→    /// Compare all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    22→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    23→    pub workspace: bool,
    24→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) (defaults to tspec* pattern)
    25→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    26→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    27→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    28→    #[arg(short, long)]
    29→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    30→}
    31→
    32→impl Execute for CompareCmd {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    34→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    35→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    36→            None
    37→        } else {
    38→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    39→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    40→                None => current_package_name(),
    41→            }
    42→        };
    43→
    44→        match resolved {
    45→            None => {
    46→                if !self.tspec.is_empty() {
    47→                    anyhow::bail!(
    48→                        "-t/--tspec cannot be used in all-packages mode. Each package uses its own tspecs."
    49→                    );
    50→                }
    51→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    52→                let results = compare_all(&workspace, self.fail_fast);
    53→                Ok(print_compare_summary(&results))
    54→            }
    55→            Some(pkg_name) => {
    56→                let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&pkg_name))?;
    57→                let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    58→                let spec_paths = if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    59→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec).unwrap_or_default()
    60→                } else {
    61→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?
    62→                };
    63→                let results = compare_specs(&pkg_name, &spec_paths)?;
    64→                print_comparison(&pkg_name, &results);
    65→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    66→            }
    67→        }
    68→    }
    69→}
    70→
    71→#[cfg(test)]
    72→mod tests {
    73→    use super::*;

    83→            _ => panic!("expected Compare command"),
    84→        }
    85→    }
    86→

    97→    }
    98→
    99→    #[test]
   100→    fn tspec_empty_by_default() {

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/.claude/plans/curried-doodling-harp.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Add glob support for `-t` flag in build, run, and test commands\n\n## Context\n\nBuild, run, and test already have `-w` (workspace mode) matching compare. The missing\npiece is that `-t`/`--tspec` accepts only a single spec name (`Option<String>`), while\ncompare accepts multiple patterns with glob support (`Vec<String>` with `num_args = 1..`).\n\nGoal: make `-t` accept glob patterns for build/run/test. When multiple specs match,\nexecute the operation once per spec in sequence (like compare does for size comparison).\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1. Update `-t` flag type in all three commands\n\n**Files:** `src/cmd/build.rs`, `src/cmd/run.rs`, `src/cmd/test.rs`\n\nChange `tspec: Option<String>` to `tspec: Vec<String>` with `num_args = 1..` (same as\ncompare). Update doc comment to mention glob support.\n\n### 2. Update single-package mode to loop over specs\n\nIn each command's `execute()`, when a package is resolved (single-package mode):\n\n- If `tspec` is empty → call `build_package`/`test_package`/`build_package+run_binary`\n  once with `None` (current default behavior).\n- If `tspec` is non-empty → resolve via `find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)`,\n  then loop over each spec path, calling the operation with\n  `Some(path.to_str().unwrap())`.\n\nThis mirrors how compare resolves specs in single-package mode (lines 56-62 of\n`src/cmd/compare.rs`).\n\n### 3. Update all-packages mode\n\nFor all-packages mode (`-w` or workspace root), apply the same rule as compare:\nreject `-t` with an error message. Each package uses its own tspecs automatically.\n\nCurrently build/run/test pass `self.tspec.as_deref()` to `build_all`/`test_all`/`run_all`.\nWith `Vec<String>`, we need to either:\n- Block `-t` in all-packages mode (like compare does) — **preferred**, keeps it simple\n- Or pass specs through to each package\n\nCompare blocks it with: `\"-t/--tspec cannot be used in all-packages mode. Each package\nuses its own tspecs.\"` — we'll do the same.\n\n### 4. Update `_all()` functions in `all.rs`\n\n**File:** `src/all.rs`\n\n`build_all`, `test_all`, `run_all` currently take `tspec: Option<&str>`. Since we're\nblocking `-t` in all-packages mode, these functions will always receive `None`. We can\neither keep the parameter (for future use) or remove it. **Keep it** — minimal change,\nno breakage.\n\n### 5. Add imports\n\nBuild, run, and test will need `find_tspecs`, `resolve_package_dir`, `get_package_name`\nfrom `crate::find_paths` (same as compare uses).\n\n### 6. Add clap parsing tests\n\nAdd tests to each command module verifying `-t` accepts multiple values and globs,\nmatching the pattern in `src/cmd/compare.rs` tests.\n\n## Files to modify\n\n- `src/cmd/build.rs` — flag type, execute logic, tests\n- `src/cmd/run.rs` — flag type, execute logic, tests\n- `src/cmd/test.rs` — flag type, execute logic, tests\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec\ntspec test -p tspec-build\ntspec install --path .\ntspec test -p tspec\ntspec clippy\ntspec fmt --check\n```\n\nManual smoke tests:\n```bash\ntspec build -t 'tspec.*.ts.toml'     # should build with each matching spec\ntspec build .                         # unchanged default behavior\ntspec build -w                        # unchanged all-packages mode\ntspec build -w -t foo                 # should error\n```\n"
}
```

{
  "plan": "# Plan: Add glob support for `-t` flag in build, run, and test commands\n\n## Context\n\nBuild, run, and test already have `-w` (workspace mode) matching compare. The missing\npiece is that `-t`/`--tspec` accepts only a single spec name (`Option<String>`), while\ncompare accepts multiple patterns with glob support (`Vec<String>` with `num_args = 1..`).\n\nGoal: make `-t` accept glob patterns for build/run/test. When multiple specs match,\nexecute the operation once per spec in sequence (like compare does for size comparison).\n\n## Step 1: Update `-t` flag type in all three commands\n\n**Files:** `src/cmd/build.rs`, `src/cmd/run.rs`, `src/cmd/test.rs`\n\nChange `tspec: Option<String>` to `tspec: Vec<String>` with `num_args = 1..` (same as\ncompare's definition at `src/cmd/compare.rs:25`). Update doc comment to mention glob.\n\n## Step 2: Update single-package execute logic\n\nIn each command's `execute()`, when a package is resolved (single-package mode):\n\n- If `tspec` is empty → call once with `None` (unchanged default behavior)\n- If `tspec` is non-empty → resolve via `find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)`\n  (reuse from `crate::find_paths`), then loop over each spec path\n\nThis mirrors compare's single-package mode (`src/cmd/compare.rs:55-64`). Each command\nneeds to import `find_tspecs`, `resolve_package_dir`, `get_package_name` from\n`crate::find_paths`.\n\n**Build:** Loop calls `build_package(name, Some(spec_path_str), release)` per spec.\nStrip if requested after each build.\n\n**Test:** Loop calls `test_package(name, Some(spec_path_str), release)` per spec.\n\n**Run:** Loop calls build + optional strip + `run_binary()` per spec.      1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It configures builds at the **package** level (one tspec per Cargo package) via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
     8→
     9→**Repository structure:** This is a Cargo workspace with a root package (`tspec`) and one member (`tspec-build`). The root `Cargo.toml` has both `[workspace]` and `[package]` sections — no subdirectory restructuring needed.
    10→
    11→- **tspec** — the main CLI binary
    12→- **tspec-build** — a library crate that packages can use in their `build.rs` to read tspec settings at build time via the `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` env var
    13→
    14→## Build Commands — Dogfood tspec
    15→
    16→**Always use `tspec` itself for development.** Never use bare `cargo test`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt`, or `cargo install` — use the tspec equivalents. This is how we catch real-world issues.
    17→
    18→```bash
    19→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tspec tests (NEVER cargo test)
    20→tspec test -p tspec-build      # Run tspec-build tests
    21→tspec clippy                   # Run lints (NEVER cargo clippy)
    22→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting (NEVER cargo fmt --check)
    23→tspec install --path .         # Install from local source (NEVER cargo install)
    24→```
    25→
    26→**After code changes pass tests, immediately install the new version** with `tspec install --path .` so subsequent commands use the latest binary. Do not defer installation — the sooner we run the real binary, the sooner we catch issues.
    27→
    28→## Architecture
    29→
    30→### Execute Trait Pattern
    31→
    32→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    33→
    34→```rust
    35→pub trait Execute {
    36→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    37→}
    38→
    39→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    40→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    41→```
    42→
    43→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    44→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    45→
    46→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    47→```rust
    48→enum Commands {
    49→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    50→    Test(TestCmd),
    51→    // ...
    52→}
    53→```
    54→
    55→### Key Modules
    56→
    57→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    58→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, `execute_cargo_subcommand` helper, `resolve_package_arg()` (resolves paths like "." to package names), `current_package_name()` (workspace-aware), re-exports
    59→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    60→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    61→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    62→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
    63→- `cargo_build.rs` - Package build orchestration with spec application
    64→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace package discovery
    65→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    66→- `ts_cmd/edit.rs` - `toml_edit` helpers: field registry, `set_field()`, `unset_field()`, `add_items()`, `remove_items_by_value()`, `remove_item_by_index()`, validation
    67→- `ts_cmd/set.rs` - `ts set` command (scalar or replace entire array)
    68→- `ts_cmd/unset.rs` - `ts unset` command (remove field entirely)
    69→- `ts_cmd/add.rs` - `ts add` command (append or insert at position)
    70→- `ts_cmd/remove.rs` - `ts remove` command (by value or by index)
    71→- `tspec-build/` - Library crate for build.rs integration (reads `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` env var)
    72→
    73→### Three Write Strategies
    74→
    75→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    76→|---|---|---|
    77→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    78→| set, unset, add, remove | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    79→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    80→
    81→### Translation Spec Structure
    82→
    83→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    84→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    85→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    86→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    87→
    88→## Conventions
    89→
    90→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    91→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    92→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    93→- **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
    94→- **Package argument pattern:** All commands accept both `-p <name-or-path>` and a positional `<PACKAGE>` argument. Paths (like `.`) are resolved to the actual cargo package name via `resolve_package_arg()`. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` resolves to None (all-packages mode). Resolution order: positional > `-p` > current directory > all packages.
    95→- **`cmd` vs `cmd .` for passthroughs:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), but `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    96→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    97→
    98→## Feature Workflow
    99→
   100→**Before starting feature or fix work:**
   101→1. Create a branch: `git checkout -b <type>-<short-description>` (e.g., `fix-compare-optional-p`)
   102→2. Create a dated entry in `notes/chores-N.md` with context and plan
   103→3. Update `notes/todo.md` to move items to In Progress
   104→4. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-dev` in `Cargo.toml`
   105→5. Commit the above as a chore marker commit before starting code changes
   106→6. Use todo list to track progress during implementation
   107→
   108→**On completion:**
   109→1. Update `notes/chores-N.md` with result
   110→2. Move items from In Progress to Done in `notes/todo.md`
   111→3. Remove `-dev` from version in `Cargo.toml`
   112→4. Commit as a release chore (include `Cargo.lock` if changed)
   113→
   114→**Branch naming:** `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`
   115→
   116→## Verification Workflow
   117→
   118→**After code changes, run verification and install immediately:**
   119→```bash
   120→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tspec tests to verify changes with "old" binary; if all pass:
   121→tspec test -p tspec-build      # Run tspec-build tests
   122→tspec install --path .         # Install ASAP so we dogfood the new binary
   123→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests again with the new binary to catch issues early
   124→tspec clippy
   125→tspec fmt --check
   126→```
   127→
   128→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
   129→```
   130→Committed abc123.
   131→
   132→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
   133→```
   134→
   135→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
   136→
   137→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
   138→
   139→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
   140→
   141→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   142→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   143→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   144→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   145→
   146→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   147→
```

```
     1→# Notes
     2→
     3→This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project. Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
     4→
     5→By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
     6→about maintenance tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
     7→
     8→As the number of chores files increase they should be put archived in /notes/chores/
     9→although links will need to be updated. I expect we may want to create a "notes"
    10→database in the furture to better manage the information, TBD.
    11→
    12→Examples chore file:
    13→```
    14→# Chores-1.md
    15→ 
    16→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for more specific topics. Chores generally don't neeed detailed explanations or dicussions, they can but if the expand to much a speararte file should be created.
    17→
    18→## 20260202 - Should tspec augment or replace cargo
    19→
    20→> Tspec could either augment cargo by providing additional functionality on top of it, or it could replace cargo entirely by offering a new way to manage Rust projects. The decision depends on the specific needs of the project and the desired user experience.
    21→```
    22→
    23→
    24→## Versioning during development
    25→
    26→For simple changes a single commit is fine. For larger changes, use a multi-step
    27→process:
    28→
    29→1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
    30→2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
    31→3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
    32→
    33→The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
    34→prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
    35→commits were exploratory vs final.
    36→
    37→## Todo format
    38→
    39→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
    40→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
    41→references.
    42→
    43→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    44→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
    45→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
    46→
    47→Examples:
    48→
    49→# Todo
    50→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
    51→- Fix bug Y [1]
    52→
    53→# Done
    54→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
    55→
    56→[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
    57→[2]: issues.md#issue-z
    58→[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
```
